1	"""Path-safety helpers for the API boundary (untrusted request fields)."""
2	import os
3	from typing import Optional
4	
5	
6	def safe_output_filename(name: str) -> str:
7	    """Validate an output filename is a BARE name (no dir part, no traversal, not absolute).
8	
9	    `os.path.join(output_dir, name)` silently discards `output_dir` when `name` is absolute,
10	    and `..` segments escape it — so an unchecked `output_filename` is an arbitrary-write
11	    primitive. Returns the name unchanged when safe; raises ValueError otherwise.
12	    """
13	    if not name or not name.strip():
14	        raise ValueError("output_filename must not be empty")
15	    if "\x00" in name:
16	        raise ValueError("output_filename must not contain a null byte")
17	    if "/" in name or "\\" in name:
18	        raise ValueError(f"output_filename must not contain a path separator: {name!r}")
19	    if os.path.isabs(name) or name != os.path.basename(name) or name in (".", ".."):
20	        raise ValueError(f"output_filename must be a bare filename, got: {name!r}")
21	    return name
22	
23	
24	def resolve_under_root(path: str, root: str) -> str:
25	    """Return the real path, asserting it is contained under `root` (raises ValueError if not)."""
26	    rp = os.path.realpath(path)
27	    rr = os.path.realpath(root)
28	    try:
29	        contained = os.path.commonpath([rp, rr]) == rr
30	    except ValueError:  # different drives on Windows
31	        contained = False
32	    if not contained:
33	        raise ValueError(f"path {path!r} escapes the allowed root {root!r}")
34	    return rp
35	
36	
37	def validate_input_path(path: str, allowed_root: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
38	    """Reject null bytes and (when an allowed_root is configured) out-of-root inputs.
39	
40	    With no allowed_root (default, single-user local mode) only the null-byte check applies,
41	    so the local 'point it at any of my videos' workflow is preserved. Setting an
42	    allowed_root (a future hosted/tenant deployment) enforces containment.
43	    """
44	    if "\x00" in path:
45	        raise ValueError("video_path must not contain a null byte")
46	    if allowed_root:
47	        resolve_under_root(path, allowed_root)
48

### 141. user (2026-06-21T07:44:45.242Z)

40	
41	class JobWaiting(Exception):
42	    """Raised inside the pipeline to SELF-SCHEDULE a job (EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3,
43	    WAIT_AND_RESUME). It means "I can't finish now — park me and resume in `delay_sec`",
44	    NOT a failure. The worker persists `next_poll_at` + `progress` (via `set_job_waiting`),
45	    releases the thread, and re-claims the job when due (`claim_due_job`). No work to date
46	    is the segmenter's `_execute_processing`, run UNDER the destroy-after-confirm contract.
47	
48	    The wiring is additive: the production segmenter path never raises this today (zero
49	    behaviour change), so a job runs exactly as before. The hook is what a later slice
50	    (resumable AI handoff) raises once a long op exhausts its in-process wait budget.
51	    """
52	
53	    def __init__(self, delay_sec: float, progress: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
54	        super().__init__(f"job parked for {delay_sec:.0f}s")
55	        self.delay_sec = max(0.0, float(delay_sec))
56	        self.progress = progress
57	
58	
59	def _job_to_request(job: Dict[str, Any]):
60	    """Reconstruct the submit-time ProcessRequest from a claimed job row, so a job
61	    re-claimed by `claim_due_job` (queued OR resumed-from-waiting) runs identically to
62	    the original submit. The row stores exactly the ProcessRequest fields."""
63	    import backend.main as _main
64	    return _main.ProcessRequest(
65	        video_path=job["video_path"],
66	        player_pool=job.get("player_pool"),
67	        max_frames=job.get("max_frames"),
68	        segmenter=job.get("segmenter"),
69	    )
70	
71	
72	def _run_claimed_job(job: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
73	    """Run ONE job already claimed (`status='running'`) by `claim_due_job`, recording its
74	    terminal state — OR parking it for resume on `JobWaiting` (WAIT_AND_RESUME).
75	
76	    Job `status` is the EXECUTION state (queued|waiting|running|done|failed): 'done' means
77	    the worker finished — the pipeline's own verdict (validation failure etc.) lives in
78	    result["status"]; 'failed' means the worker itself crashed/raised; 'waiting' means the
79	    job self-scheduled and will be re-claimed when `next_poll_at` is due.
80	    """
81	    import backend.main as _main
82	    db_instance = _main.db_instance
83	    job_id = job["id"]
84	    req = _main._job_to_request(job)
85	    try:
86	        result = _main._execute_processing(
87	            req, progress=lambda stage: db_instance.set_job_progress(job_id, stage))
88	        if result.get("video_id"):
89	            db_instance.set_job_video_id(job_id, result["video_id"])
90	        db_instance.mark_job_done(job_id, result)
91	    except _main.JobWaiting as w:
92	        # Cooperative re-enqueue: persist progress + next_poll_at, release the worker.
93	        logger.info(f"Job {job_id} parked for {w.delay_sec:.0f}s (resume when due).")
94	        db_instance.set_job_waiting(job_id, w.delay_sec, progress=w.progress)
95	    except Exception as e:
96	        logger.error(f"Job {job_id} crashed: {e}\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
97	        db_instance.mark_job_failed(job_id, f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}")
98	
99

152	        return _P()

identity passthrough for every input path used today (no fetch, no copy). A bucket/Drive source is
routed through storage.fetch to a local scratch file. All offline (the remote backend is a
monkeypatched fetch — no boto3/google-cloud-storage, no network).

OS note: os.path.isabs is platform-dependent, so the absolute-path cases use a POSIX-style
'/data/...' (absolute on BOTH Windows and POSIX) and Windows backslash paths only with input_root=''
(branch 5, OS-independent) — the full suite runs cross-OS in CI.
"""
import os

import pytest

from backend import storage
from backend.storage import acquire_local_input, input_is_local
from backend.storage.ref import resolve_input_uri


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- resolve_input_uri
@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw,ib,ir,expected", [
    # 1. explicit scheme always wins (even over a non-local backend + root)
    ("gs://b/x.mp4", "local", "", "gs://b/x.mp4"),
    ("s3://b/x.mp4", "gcs", "gs://r", "s3://b/x.mp4"),
    ("local://abs/x.mp4", "gcs", "gs://r", "local://abs/x.mp4"),
    ("gdrive://Sharing/x.mp4", "local", "", "gdrive://Sharing/x.mp4"),
    # 2. an absolute local path is always local, never joined (POSIX abs = abs on all OSes)
    ("/data/x.mp4", "gcs", "gs://r", "/data/x.mp4"),
    # 3. a bare/relative name joins under a non-empty input_root
    ("x.mp4", "local", "gs://bucket/videos", "gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4"),
    ("sub/x.mp4", "local", "gs://bucket/videos/", "gs://bucket/videos/sub/x.mp4"),  # root trailing slash
    ("/lead.mp4", "local", "s3://b/p", "s3://b/p/lead.mp4"),  # joined leading slash collapses
    # 4. input_backend prefixes a bare bucket/key when no input_root
    ("bucket/x.mp4", "gcs", "", "gcs://bucket/x.mp4"),
    ("bucket/x.mp4", "s3", "", "s3://bucket/x.mp4"),
    # 5. bare/relative local path — today's behaviour, unchanged
    ("x.mp4", "local", "", "x.mp4"),
    ("rel/sub/x.mp4", "local", "", "rel/sub/x.mp4"),
    ("C:\\v\\x.mp4", "local", "", "C:\\v\\x.mp4"),  # Windows path, default config: unchanged (OS-independent)
])
def test_resolve_input_uri(raw, ib, ir, expected):
    assert resolve_input_uri(raw, input_backend=ib, input_root=ir) == expected


def test_resolve_input_uri_default_is_identity():
    for p in ("/data/clip.mp4", "rel/clip.mp4", "clip.mp4", "gs://b/k", "local://x"):
        assert resolve_input_uri(p) == p


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- input_is_local
def test_input_is_local_true_for_local_paths():
    assert input_is_local("/data/x.mp4") is True
    assert input_is_local("rel/x.mp4") is True
    assert input_is_local("local://x.mp4") is True


def test_input_is_local_false_for_buckets():
    assert input_is_local("gs://b/x.mp4") is False
    assert input_is_local("s3://b/x.mp4") is False
    assert input_is_local("gdrive://Sharing/x.mp4") is False


def test_input_is_local_follows_input_root():
    cfg = {"input_backend": "local", "input_root": "gs://bucket/videos"}
    assert input_is_local("x.mp4", cfg) is False           # bare name resolves into the bucket
    assert input_is_local("/abs/x.mp4", cfg) is True        # absolute path stays local
    assert input_is_local("gs://other/x.mp4", cfg) is False


def test_input_is_local_accepts_pydantic_storage_config():
    from backend.config.models import StorageConfig
    sc = StorageConfig(input_root="gs://bucket/videos")
    assert input_is_local("x.mp4", sc) is False
    assert input_is_local("/abs/x.mp4", sc) is True


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- acquire_local_input
def test_acquire_local_input_local_identity(tmp_path):
    p = tmp_path / "v.mp4"
    p.write_bytes(b"abc")
    # Identity passthrough — returns the SAME string, no copy, no temp dir.
    assert acquire_local_input(str(p)) == str(p)


def test_acquire_local_input_relative_local_identity():
    assert acquire_local_input("rel/clip.mp4") == "rel/clip.mp4"


def _capturing_fetch(captured):
    def _f(uri, dst=None, *, config=None, overwrite=False):
        captured["uri"] = uri
        captured["dst"] = dst
        captured["config"] = config
        if dst:
            with open(dst, "wb") as fh:
                fh.write(b"remote-bytes")
        return dst or uri
    return _f


def test_acquire_local_input_remote_fetches_to_scratch(monkeypatch):
    captured = {}
    monkeypatch.setattr(storage, "fetch", _capturing_fetch(captured))
    out = acquire_local_input("gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4", {"backend": "local", "gcs": {}})
    assert captured["uri"] == "gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4"          # exact URI routed to fetch
    assert os.path.basename(out) == "x.mp4"                       # named from the ref
    assert os.path.isfile(out) and open(out, "rb").read() == b"remote-bytes"
    # The whole storage settings dict is forwarded so per-backend (gcs/s3) config reaches fetch.
    assert captured["config"] == {"backend": "local", "gcs": {}}


def test_acquire_local_input_input_root_joins_then_fetches(monkeypatch):
    captured = {}
    monkeypatch.setattr(storage, "fetch", _capturing_fetch(captured))
    acquire_local_input("x.mp4", {"input_root": "gs://bucket/videos"})
    assert captured["uri"] == "gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4"          # resolved BEFORE fetch


def test_acquire_local_input_never_fetches_for_local(monkeypatch):
    def _boom(*a, **k):
        raise AssertionError("fetch must not be called for a local input")
    monkeypatch.setattr(storage, "fetch", _boom)
    assert acquire_local_input("/data/x.mp4") == "/data/x.mp4"
    assert acquire_local_input("clip.mp4", {"input_backend": "local", "input_root": ""}) == "clip.mp4"

299	            # falls back to the duration-based estimate (no AI exchange count).

301	                {
302	                    "start_time": all_candidate_windows[wi]["start"],
303	                    "end_time": all_candidate_windows[wi]["end"],
304	                    "shuttle_exchange_count": None,

306	                    "_candidate_id": candidate_ids[wi],
307	                }
308	                for wi in handoff_indices
309	            ]
310	            logger.info("Phase 3 SKIPPED (fully-local): emitted %d candidate windows as rallies "
311	                        "(no AI handoff).", len(ai_segments))
312	        else:
313	            handoff_dir = os.path.join("output", f"chunks_{provider_name}_handoff")
314	            os.makedirs(handoff_dir, exist_ok=True)
315	            logger.info("Phase 3: %s validation on %d candidate windows...",
316	                        provider_name, len(handoff_indices))
317	
318	            def process_candidate_window(wi: int, window: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[dict]:
319	                w_start = max(0, window["start"] - handoff_padding)
320	                w_end = min(video_duration, window["end"] + handoff_padding)
321	                w_dur = w_end - w_start
322	                w_path = os.path.join(handoff_dir, f"handoff_{video_id}_{wi}.mp4")

21	    in-flight work. A per-video subdir means each run only ever creates/cleans its own.
22	    """

### 309. assistant (2026-06-21T08:27:24.293Z)

"""Configurable output/scratch base (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M3).

The segmenters' chunk-staging / AI-handoff / temp dirs no longer hardcode ``./output`` — they
root under the configured ``output.output_dir`` (default ``"output"``, so byte-identical to the
prior literal; a custom ``--output-dir`` / ``output.output_dir`` now actually moves the scratch).
"""
import os
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch

from backend.config.models import AppConfig
from backend.pipeline.segmenters.gemini import _chunk_workdir
from backend.pipeline.segmenters.wasb_hybrid import WasbHybridSegmenter
from backend.utils.paths import output_base


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- output_base() helper
def test_output_base_default_is_output():
    assert output_base(AppConfig()) == "output"          # typed config, built-in default
    assert output_base({"output": {"output_dir": "output"}}) == "output"


def test_output_base_honors_custom_output_dir():
    cfg = AppConfig()
    cfg.output.output_dir = "/tmp/custom_out"
    assert output_base(cfg) == "/tmp/custom_out"
    assert output_base({"output": {"output_dir": "reels"}}) == "reels"  # legacy dict-shim


def test_output_base_falls_back_to_output_on_missing_or_empty():
    assert output_base({}) == "output"                   # no output section
    assert output_base({"output": {}}) == "output"       # no output_dir key
    assert output_base({"output": {"output_dir": ""}}) == "output"  # empty -> default
    assert output_base(None) == "output"                 # defensive: bad config


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- _chunk_workdir base
def test_chunk_workdir_default_base_is_byte_identical():
    # The no-arg call (used by tests / any legacy caller) is unchanged.
    assert _chunk_workdir("vidA") == os.path.join("output", "chunks", "vidA")


def test_chunk_workdir_honors_custom_base():
    assert _chunk_workdir("vidA", "/tmp/out") == os.path.join("/tmp/out", "chunks", "vidA")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- segmenter call-site
def _mock_runner():
    runner = MagicMock()
    runner.healthcheck.return_value = (True, "env ok")
    runner.run_predict.return_value = "out.csv"
    runner.parse_trajectory_csv.return_value = [MagicMock(visible=True)]
    runner.trajectory_to_action_windows.return_value = [{
        "start": 1.0, "end": 4.0, "active_frame_count": 30,
        "peak_velocity": 0.4, "mean_velocity": 0.2, "track_id_count": 1, "chunk_index": 0,
    }]
    return runner


def _run_wasb_capture_out_dir(config):
    """Run wasb_hybrid through the standard mocked pipeline; return the out_dir passed to
    run_predict (Phase 2 trajectory dir)."""
    runner = _mock_runner()
    db = MagicMock()
    db.add_candidate_segment.return_value = 55
    db.add_play_segment.return_value = 200
    provider = MagicMock()
    provider.analyze_video.return_value = (
        [{"start_time": 0.5, "end_time": 3.0, "shuttle_exchange_count": 4}], {})
    with patch.object(WasbHybridSegmenter, "_build_runner", return_value=(runner, {"weights_path": "w"})), \
         patch.object(WasbHybridSegmenter, "_probe_fps_width", return_value=(30.0, 1280.0)), \
         patch("backend.pipeline.segmenters.trajectory_hybrid.get_video_duration", return_value=30.0), \
         patch("backend.pipeline.segmenters.trajectory_hybrid.extract_video_chunk", return_value=True), \
         patch("backend.pipeline.segmenters.trajectory_hybrid.provider_registry") as preg, \
         patch("os.makedirs"), patch("os.environ.get", return_value="key"):
        preg.get.return_value = provider
        seg = WasbHybridSegmenter(db, config)
        seg.process_video("v1", "clip.mp4")
    return runner.run_predict.call_args[0][1]  # 2nd positional arg = out_dir


def test_trajectory_scratch_default_is_literal_output():
    """Default config: out_dir is `output/wasb` — byte-identical to the prior hardcode."""
    assert _run_wasb_capture_out_dir({"indexing": {}}) == os.path.join("output", "wasb")


def test_trajectory_scratch_follows_custom_output_dir(tmp_path):
    """A configured output_dir actually moves the trajectory scratch (the latent bug M3 fixes)."""
    custom = str(tmp_path / "custom_out")
    out_dir = _run_wasb_capture_out_dir({"indexing": {}, "output": {"output_dir": custom}})
    assert out_dir == os.path.join(custom, "wasb")

---

## M3 Review — COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING: configurable output/scratch base

**Verdict: CLEAN BILL — ship it.** No blockers, no majors. M3 does exactly what it claims: routes the 6 hardcoded scratch/staging/temp sites through `output_base(config)`, defaults to `"output"` byte-identically, and is effectively default-OFF (only a non-default `--output-dir` moves anything). All 7 new tests + 199 segmenter/config/path tests pass. Two minor/nit observations on test coverage and a doc nuance, no fix required to merge.

### 1. Byte-identical default — PASS
- `output_base()` reads `output.output_dir` (`backend/utils/paths.py:16`), which defaults to `"output"` (`OutputConfig.output_dir`, `backend/config/models.py:233`).
- In the real pipeline the segmenter receives the typed `global_config` (`backend/main.py:212,646`), and `--output-dir` is written to the **same** field `output.output_dir` (`backend/main.py:580`) that already governs the DB path, reels, and clips. So M3's scratch now follows the identical knob as everything else — that's the latent bug it fixes, and the default path is unchanged.
- No code path returns a non-`"output"` base by default: missing `output` section, missing key, empty string, `None` config, and a non-dict `output` value all fall back to `"output"` via `out or "output"` + the bare `except` (`paths.py:15-19`). Verified by `test_output_base_falls_back_to_output_on_missing_or_empty`.

### 2. Create/cleanup consistency — PASS
Every makedirs/rmdir pair uses the **same local variable**, computed once from `output_base(self.config)`, so cleanup can never point at a literal `"output"` while creation used a custom base:
- `yolo_hybrid.py`: `gemini_dir` (270→314), `chunks_dir` (409→424).
- `trajectory_hybrid.py`: `handoff_dir` (315→350). `out_dir` (238) has no rmdir here — the `DetectorRunner` owns it; M3 introduces no orphan.
- `gemini.py`: `chunks_dir` threaded 540→186(makedirs)→314(rmdir); `base`/`temp_trimmed_path` (124→127/152). All consistent.

`output_base` is a pure function of `self.config`, so create and cleanup always derive the identical base within a run. No orphan / failing-rmdir risk.

### 3. Missed sites — PASS
The only remaining `"output"` literal anywhere in `backend/pipeline/segmenters/` is the intentional `base="output"` default param of `_chunk_workdir` (`gemini.py:17`), which is byte-identical-by-design. No `tempfile`/`mkdtemp`/`"./"`/other `os.path.join("output", …)` literals remain in the 3 files. `WasbHybridSegmenter` subclasses `TrajectoryHybridSegmenter` with no scratch literals of its own, so it inherits the fixed site. (`rally_seg_eval.py` DEFAULT_MANIFEST correctly ignored per scope.)

### 4. Config access — PASS
`config.get("output", {}).get("output_dir")` works for both shapes:
- **Typed AppConfig**: `.get("output", {})` hits `model_fields`, and since the section is a `BaseModel` it is returned as a plain dict via `model_dump(mode="json")` (`models.py:402-403`) — so the chained `.get("output_dir")` works. Confirmed by `test_output_base_default_is_output(AppConfig())` and `..._honors_custom_output_dir`.
- **Legacy dict**: ordinary nested `.get`.
- No uncaught raise: any AttributeError (e.g. `output` is a string in a legacy dict) is swallowed by the bare `except Exception` → `"output"`.

### 5. Test gaps — minor
- **Minor — only the wasb/trajectory call site is path-pinned.** `tests/test_output_base.py` pins both default (`output/wasb`) and custom (`<custom>/wasb`) at a *real* segmenter call (`_run_wasb_capture_out_dir`, lines 81-90), which is solid. But the `yolo_hybrid` sites (`chunks_yolo`, `chunks_{provider}_handoff`) and the `gemini` `temp_trimmed`/handoff sites get **no path assertion** — only `_chunk_workdir` is unit-tested in isolation. The pre-existing `test_yolo_hybrid.py`/`test_gemini.py` pass dict configs with no `output` section and assert nothing about the path (they patch `os.makedirs`), so those new sites have no-crash execution coverage but not custom-base coverage. Risk is low (all sites use the identical one-liner), but a future regression on the yolo/gemini sites would not be caught. Fix (optional): add one yolo-style capture test mirroring `_run_wasb_capture_out_dir` asserting `chunks_yolo` follows a custom `output_dir`.
- **Nit — the trajectory `out_dir` is tested only via `run_predict`'s 2nd positional arg.** Fine and intentional, just noting the test depends on that arg position (`call_args[0][1]`); a signature change to `run_predict` would silently mis-target. Acceptable.

**Net:** correct, well-scoped, byte-identical-by-default, create/cleanup-coherent. The only gap is asymmetric test depth (wasb pinned, yolo/gemini not) — a minor coverage nit, not a correctness defect.

### 370. user (2026-06-21T08:45:42.412Z)

1	"""Linux-native WASB shuttle-trajectory runner (no WSL).
2	
3	The de-risk goal the ``DetectorRunner`` ABC was built for (see ``base.py`` —
4	"Optional Linux-native path"; ``docs/DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/04-BUILD-AND-TEST-PLAN.md`` M3.5).
5	It is the sibling of :class:`~backend.pipeline.detectors.wasb_runner.WasbRunner` for a
6	Linux GPU box (GCP / a SaaS worker): it runs the **same** verified ``wasb_infer.py`` core
7	that the WSL adapter runs, so the trajectory CSV is identical (to a sub-pixel float tolerance;
8	see the acceptance gate below) — but WITHOUT the WSL transport:
9	
10	  * NO ``WslCommandMixin`` (no ``wsl -d <distro> bash -c``), NO ``conda activate`` (the
11	    ``wasb`` env's python is invoked **by path**), NO ``/mnt/c`` path translation
12	    (``to_wsl_mnt_path``). Paths are native; the video is already local on the box.
13	  * ``device`` is ``cuda`` (default — the WSL parity path), ``mps`` or ``cpu``; threaded
14	    into ``wasb_infer.py --device`` (and so into the Hydra ``runner.device`` override).
15	  * weights / repo / python / scratch come from the **environment** first
16	    (``WASB_WEIGHTS`` / ``WASB_REPO_DIR`` / ``WASB_PYTHON`` / ``WASB_SCRATCH``), then
17	    ``indexing.wasb.*`` config, then a default — so a box needs no config edits.
18	
19	It emits the identical ``Frame,Visibility,X,Y`` CSV (same ``{stem}__{vid12}_wasb.csv``
20	name as the WSL runner) and reuses the model-agnostic ``parse_trajectory_csv`` /
21	``trajectory_to_action_windows`` from ``tracknet_runner``, so the trajectory hybrids and
22	the windowing brain are unchanged. Selected via the existing ``detector_impl`` seam
23	(``trajectory_hybrid._resolve_runner`` → ``_build_native_runner``); ``detector_impl="native"``.
24	
25	**Acceptance gate (parity, NOT strict byte-equality):** the SAME clip through the WSL runner
26	(Windows) and this runner (Linux, ``device=cuda``) yields the same trajectory CSV + the same
27	candidate windows **within a small float tolerance (~1e-3 px)**. cuDNN is non-deterministic
28	ACROSS GPUs, so a sub-pixel diff is expected on different hardware — but it is byte-exact
29	run-to-run on a FIXED GPU. ✅ Validated 2026-06-20 on an RTX 2070 (via WSL): native streaming
30	vs the cached WSL-disk CSV matched **1194/1195** frames (the lone diff a ~5e-5 px edge
31	artifact), and two native runs were byte-identical (deterministic). This is a hardware check
32	(a real GPU + the ``wasb`` env), so the offline, subprocess-mocked suite asserts the contract,
33	not the numbers.
34	"""
35	import os
36	import shutil
37	import logging
38	import subprocess
39	from dataclasses import dataclass
40	from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
41	
42	from backend.pipeline.detectors.base import DetectorRunner
43	# Reuse the model-agnostic helpers — trajectory shape + windowing are identical to WSL/TrackNet.
44	from backend.pipeline.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrackNetRunner
45	
46	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
47	
48	# Path to the inference core we copy into the WASB repo's src/ (so it can import WASB's
49	# detectors/trackers/dataloaders + find configs/), exactly as the WSL adapter does.
50	_INFER_PY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "wasb_infer.py")
51	
52	
53	@dataclass
54	class NativeWasbConfig:
55	    """Resolved settings for a Linux-native WASB run.
56	
57	    Built via :meth:`from_indexing_cfg` with **environment overrides** taking precedence
58	    over ``indexing.wasb.*`` config (the box sets env; no config edits needed). ``device``
59	    defaults to ``cuda`` (the WSL parity path); ``stream_video`` defaults to True (the perf win).
60	    """
61	    repo_dir: str = "~/models/WASB-SBDT"   # native clone of nttcom/WASB-SBDT
62	    python_bin: str = "python"             # the `wasb` conda env python (invoked by path, no activate)
63	    weights_path: str = ""                 # REQUIRED (env WASB_WEIGHTS or indexing.wasb.weights_path)
64	    sport: str = "badminton"
65	    device: str = "cuda"                   # cuda | mps | cpu
66	    scratch_dir: str = ""                  # frame-extraction scratch (env); "" = next to the output dir
67	    timeout_sec: int = 1800               # 30 min; a hung GPU call is killed (0 = no timeout)
68	    keep_frames: bool = False              # retain the decoded frame cache after success (debug only)
69	    # NATIVE DEFAULT = STREAMING: skip the cv2 PNG extraction that dominates a long clip's
70	    # wall-clock (measured ~50s on a 20s clip) — bit-identical to disk (verified on a real GPU
71	    # 2026-06-20). The GPU box reads from a local NVMe so streaming is the right default here.
72	    stream_video: bool = True
73	
74	    @classmethod
75	    def from_indexing_cfg(cls, idx_cfg: Dict[str, Any]) -> "NativeWasbConfig":
76	        w = (idx_cfg or {}).get("wasb", {}) or {}
77	        env = os.environ.get
78	        # stream_video is tri-state in config: None/absent => native default (stream); an
79	        # explicit True/False still wins (e.g. False for a container cv2 can't seek).
80	        sv = w.get("stream_video")
81	        return cls(
82	            repo_dir=env("WASB_REPO_DIR") or w.get("repo_dir", "~/models/WASB-SBDT"),
83	            python_bin=env("WASB_PYTHON") or w.get("python_bin", "python"),
84	            weights_path=env("WASB_WEIGHTS") or w.get("weights_path", "") or "",
85	            sport=w.get("sport", "badminton"),
86	            device=env("WASB_DEVICE") or w.get("device", "cuda"),
87	            scratch_dir=env("WASB_SCRATCH") or env("RALLY_SCRATCH") or env("TMPDIR") or "",
88	            timeout_sec=int(w.get("timeout_sec", 1800)),
89	            keep_frames=bool(w.get("keep_frames", False)),
90	            stream_video=(True if sv is None else bool(sv)),
91	        )
92	
93	
94	class NativeWasbRunner(DetectorRunner):
95	    """WASB runner that executes natively on a Linux GPU box — no WSL. See module docstring."""
96	
97	    def __init__(self, cfg: NativeWasbConfig):
98	        self.cfg = cfg
99	
100	    # --- low-level native exec (the single place tests patch) --------------------------
101	    def _run(self, argv: List[str], cwd: Optional[str] = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess:
102	        logger.debug("native exec: %s (cwd=%s)", " ".join(argv), cwd)
103	        return subprocess.run(argv, cwd=cwd, capture_output=True, text=True,
104	                              timeout=(self.cfg.timeout_sec or None))
105	
106	    def _repo_src(self) -> str:
107	        return os.path.join(os.path.expanduser(self.cfg.repo_dir), "src")
108	
109	    def _copy_infer_script(self) -> bool:
110	        """Copy wasb_infer.py into the WASB repo src/ so it imports WASB modules + finds configs/."""
111	        repo_src = self._repo_src()
112	        try:
113	            os.makedirs(repo_src, exist_ok=True)
114	            shutil.copy(_INFER_PY, os.path.join(repo_src, "wasb_infer.py"))
115	            return True
116	        except OSError as e:
117	            logger.error("Failed to copy wasb_infer.py into %s: %s", repo_src, e)
118	            return False
119	
120	    def _rm_rf(self, path: Optional[str]) -> None:
121	        """Best-effort recursive delete of a native path (post-success cleanup; never raises)."""
122	        if path:
123	            shutil.rmtree(path, ignore_errors=True)
124	
125	    def healthcheck(self) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
126	        """Verify weights + repo present and the `wasb` python imports torch (and sees a GPU on cuda)."""
127	        if not self.cfg.weights_path:
128	            return False, ("WASB weights path is empty — set WASB_WEIGHTS (or indexing.wasb.weights_path).")
129	        weights = os.path.expanduser(self.cfg.weights_path)
130	        if not os.path.exists(weights):
131	            return False, f"WASB weights not found at {weights}"
132	        repo_src = self._repo_src()
133	        if not os.path.isdir(repo_src):
134	            return False, (f"WASB-SBDT repo src/ not found at {repo_src} — clone nttcom/WASB-SBDT "
135	                           f"(set WASB_REPO_DIR / indexing.wasb.repo_dir).")
136	        code = "import torch; print('torch', torch.__version__, 'cuda', torch.cuda.is_available())"
137	        try:
138	            res = self._run([self.cfg.python_bin, "-c", code])
139	        except FileNotFoundError:
140	            return False, f"python binary not found: {self.cfg.python_bin!r} (set WASB_PYTHON)."
141	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
142	            return False, "native torch healthcheck timed out."
143	        out = (res.stdout or "").strip()
144	        if res.returncode != 0:
145	            return False, f"`{self.cfg.python_bin}` torch import failed: {(res.stderr or out).strip()}"
146	        if self.cfg.device == "cuda" and "cuda True" not in out:
147	            return False, f"device=cuda but torch.cuda.is_available() is False ({out})"
148	        return True, out
149	
150	    def run_predict(self, video_win_path: str, output_win_dir: str,
151	                    video_id: Optional[str] = None) -> Optional[str]:
152	        """Run WASB natively on a video. Returns the path to the trajectory CSV, or None.
153	
154	        Output artifacts are namespaced by ``video_id`` (the file MD5) exactly as the WSL
155	        runner does, and the CSV is named ``{stem}__{vid12}_wasb.csv`` — byte-for-byte the
156	        same downstream contract, so the parity comparison is apples-to-apples.
157	        """
158	        output_dir = os.path.abspath(output_win_dir)
159	        os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
160	        # Normalize for a cross-platform basename (tests pass Windows paths on Linux).
161	        norm = str(video_win_path).replace("\\", "/")
162	        stem, _ext = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(norm))
163	        # Content-derived key: same filename + different bytes -> different key (no cache reuse).
164	        key = f"{stem}__{video_id[:12]}" if video_id else (stem or "wasb")
165	        out_csv = os.path.join(output_dir, f"{key}_wasb.csv")
166	
167	        if not self._copy_infer_script():
168	            logger.error("Could not stage wasb_infer.py into the WASB repo src/.")
169	            return None
170	
171	        weights = os.path.expanduser(self.cfg.weights_path)
172	        argv = [self.cfg.python_bin, "wasb_infer.py",
173	                "--video", os.path.abspath(str(video_win_path))]
174	        frames_dir: Optional[str] = None
175	        if self.cfg.stream_video:
176	            # Fast path: decode in-process, no PNG extraction. Output is bit-identical to disk.
177	            argv.append("--stream-video")
178	        else:
179	            scratch = self.cfg.scratch_dir or output_dir
180	            frames_dir = os.path.join(scratch, f"{key}_frames")
181	            argv += ["--frames_out_dir", frames_dir]
182	        argv += ["--weights", weights,
183	                 "--sport", self.cfg.sport,
184	                 "--device", self.cfg.device,
185	                 "--out", out_csv]
186	
187	        logger.info("Running native WASB inference (device=%s) on %s ...",
188	                    self.cfg.device, os.path.basename(norm))
189	        try:
190	            res = self._run(argv, cwd=self._repo_src())
191	        except FileNotFoundError:
192	            logger.error("python binary not found: %r (set WASB_PYTHON).", self.cfg.python_bin)
193	            return None
194	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
195	            logger.error("WASB native inference timed out after %ss.", self.cfg.timeout_sec)
196	            return None
197	        if res.returncode != 0:
198	            logger.error("WASB native inference failed:\n%s", (res.stderr or res.stdout or "")[-2000:])
199	            return None
200	        if not os.path.exists(out_csv):
201	            logger.error("WASB native finished but expected CSV not found at %s", out_csv)
202	            return None
203	        logger.info("WASB trajectory CSV: %s", out_csv)
204	
205	        # Success → the CSV is the durable output; the decoded frame cache is a pure intermediate.
206	        # On any earlier failure we return above WITHOUT cleaning, so the cache survives a resume.
207	        # (Streaming never materializes frames, so there is nothing to drop on that path.)
208	        if not self.cfg.keep_frames and frames_dir:
209	            logger.info("Cache hygiene: removing frame cache for %s "
210	                        "(set indexing.wasb.keep_frames=true to retain).", key)
211	            self._rm_rf(frames_dir)
212	        return out_csv
213	
214	    # Trajectory parsing + windowing are inherited from tracknet_runner (model-agnostic).
215	    parse_trajectory_csv = staticmethod(TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv)
216	    trajectory_to_action_windows = staticmethod(TrackNetRunner.trajectory_to_action_windows)

### 383. assistant (2026-06-21T08:58:26.914Z)

"""DeviceContext — resolve the effective torch device for a detector runner.

COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M4 (realizes the ``DeviceContext`` designed in the archived
``07-COMPUTE-ABSTRACTION``). A runner asks for a device by name; this turns the *requested*
device + a probed CUDA availability into the *effective* device to actually run on, and says
whether a CPU fallback happened.

The portability gap it closes: the native WASB runner hardcoded ``device='cuda'`` and its
healthcheck hard-failed on a box with no GPU, so a CPU-only Linux box (cpu-serving / a
no-GPU truly-local install / CI) could not run at all.

**Safety contract (so "cuda default unchanged" holds):**
  * ``"auto"``  → ``cuda`` if a GPU is available, else ``cpu`` (the fallback). This is opt-in.
  * ``"cuda"``  → honored verbatim; an *explicit* cuda request on a GPU-less box still hard-fails
    elsewhere (no silent slow-CPU downgrade of a config that asked for the GPU).
  * ``"cpu"`` / ``"mps"`` → honored verbatim.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

AUTO = "auto"
CUDA = "cuda"
CPU = "cpu"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DeviceContext:
    """The requested device + a probed CUDA availability → the effective device."""

    requested: str          # what config/env asked for: auto | cuda | mps | cpu
    cuda_available: bool     # probed in the runner's own torch env (not the main process)

    @property
    def effective(self) -> str:
        """The device the runner should actually use. ``auto`` resolves to cuda-if-available-else-cpu;
        any explicit device is returned verbatim."""
        if self.requested == AUTO:
            return CUDA if self.cuda_available else CPU
        return self.requested

    @property
    def fell_back(self) -> bool:
        """True when ``auto`` resolved to CPU because no GPU was found (worth a loud warning)."""
        return self.requested == AUTO and not self.cuda_available

    @property
    def cuda_required_but_missing(self) -> bool:
        """True when an EXPLICIT ``cuda`` request can't be satisfied — the caller should hard-fail
        rather than silently downgrade (preserving the pre-M4 strict behaviour for ``device='cuda'``)."""
        return self.requested == CUDA and not self.cuda_available

137	# --- run_predict ------------------------------------------------------------------------
138	def _drive_success(cfg, tmp_path, vid="abc123abc123", **patches):
139	    """Drive run_predict to success with the subprocess mocked; returns (runner, out, rm_spy, argv, cwd)."""
140	    r = NativeWasbRunner(cfg)
141	    key = f"clip__{vid[:12]}"
142	    (tmp_path / f"{key}_wasb.csv").write_text("Frame,Visibility,X,Y\n")  # success = CSV exists
143	    with patch.object(r, "_copy_infer_script", return_value=True), \
144	         patch.object(r, "_run", return_value=_proc(0)) as run_spy, \
145	         patch.object(r, "_rm_rf") as rm_spy:
146	        out = r.run_predict(r"C:\v\clip.mp4", str(tmp_path), video_id=vid)
147	    argv = run_spy.call_args[0][0]
148	    cwd = run_spy.call_args.kwargs.get("cwd")
149	    return r, out, rm_spy, argv, cwd, key

153	    # Pin disk mode here (default is now streaming) so this also covers the --frames_out_dir path.
154	    _, out, rm, argv, cwd, key = _drive_success(
155	        NativeWasbConfig(weights_path="/m/w.pth", device="cuda", stream_video=False), tmp_path)
156	    assert out == os.path.join(os.path.abspath(str(tmp_path)), f"{key}_wasb.csv")
157	    # No WSL / conda plumbing anywhere in the command.
158	    assert "wsl" not in argv
159	    assert not any("conda" in a or "/mnt/" in a for a in argv)
160	    # It runs the SAME inference core, device-threaded, writing the SAME CSV name as WSL.
161	    assert argv[0] == "python" and argv[1] == "wasb_infer.py"
162	    assert "--device" in argv and argv[argv.index("--device") + 1] == "cuda"
163	    assert "--weights" in argv and argv[argv.index("--weights") + 1] == "/m/w.pth"
164	    assert argv[argv.index("--out") + 1] == out
165	    # Disk mode extracts frames + cleans them on success.
166	    assert "--frames_out_dir" in argv and "--stream-video" not in argv
167	    assert cwd.endswith(os.path.join("src"))
168	    rm.assert_called_once()

### 399. assistant (2026-06-21T09:01:07.454Z)

"""DeviceContext — torch device resolution for detector runners (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M4).

Pins the safety contract: ``auto`` falls back cuda→cpu (the new portability win), while an
explicit ``cuda`` is honored verbatim and flagged as required-but-missing (the caller hard-fails,
no silent slow-CPU downgrade — "cuda default unchanged").
"""
from backend.pipeline.detectors.device import DeviceContext


def test_auto_resolves_to_cuda_when_available():
    ctx = DeviceContext("auto", cuda_available=True)
    assert ctx.effective == "cuda"
    assert ctx.fell_back is False
    assert ctx.cuda_required_but_missing is False


def test_auto_falls_back_to_cpu_when_no_gpu():
    ctx = DeviceContext("auto", cuda_available=False)
    assert ctx.effective == "cpu"
    assert ctx.fell_back is True
    assert ctx.cuda_required_but_missing is False


def test_explicit_cuda_is_honored_verbatim():
    assert DeviceContext("cuda", cuda_available=True).effective == "cuda"
    miss = DeviceContext("cuda", cuda_available=False)
    assert miss.effective == "cuda"                 # honored — NOT silently downgraded to cpu
    assert miss.cuda_required_but_missing is True    # …so the caller hard-fails
    assert miss.fell_back is False


def test_explicit_cpu_and_mps_pass_through():
    assert DeviceContext("cpu", cuda_available=False).effective == "cpu"
    assert DeviceContext("cpu", cuda_available=True).effective == "cpu"
    assert DeviceContext("mps", cuda_available=False).effective == "mps"
    for d in ("cpu", "mps"):
        ctx = DeviceContext(d, cuda_available=False)
        assert ctx.fell_back is False
        assert ctx.cuda_required_but_missing is False


def test_is_frozen():
    import dataclasses
    import pytest
    ctx = DeviceContext("auto", cuda_available=True)
    with pytest.raises(dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError):
        ctx.requested = "cpu"  # type: ignore[misc]

In healthcheck line 176: `self._cuda_available = "cuda True" in out` is set for **every** device (cuda/cpu/mps/auto) once torch imports successfully. This is a genuine probed value, so the cache is always coherent with reality. For `auto`, run_predict's `_effective_device` → `_cuda_is_available` sees the cached bool (not None) → no second probe. Correct and coherent.

One subtle thing to verify: is there any scenario where the cache is set to a **stale** value? The probe reflects the box's GPU at healthcheck time; GPU availability doesn't change within a process lifetime, so no staleness risk. Clean.

The review is complete. All checks pass.

## Review: M4 (DeviceContext + WASB CPU-fallback) — COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING

**Verdict: clean bill. No blockers, no majors. Two nits.** The "cuda default unchanged" safety contract holds, `auto` never reaches `wasb_infer.py`, the probe cache is coherent, and the safety/fallback/cache contracts are all pinned by tests that would fail on regression.

Verified locally: full suite **1125 passed** (`python run_all_tests.py`), the M4 tests **43 passed**, `ruff check` clean, `mypy` clean on both changed source files.

### 1. SAFETY / "cuda default unchanged" — PASS
- Default is still `cuda`: `backend/config/models.py:72` and `native_wasb_runner.py:67` both default `device="cuda"`; `from_indexing_cfg` falls back to `"cuda"` (`native_wasb_runner.py:88`).
- Explicit `cuda` on a GPU-less box still **hard-fails** the healthcheck: `native_wasb_runner.py:177-181` → `DeviceContext.cuda_required_but_missing` (`device.py:49-52`) is True only for `requested=="cuda" and not cuda_available`, returning the unchanged `device=cuda but torch.cuda.is_available() is False` error. Pinned by `test_healthcheck_cuda_unavailable_when_device_cuda` and `test_explicit_cuda_is_honored_verbatim`.
- No silent cuda→cpu path: `_effective_device` (`native_wasb_runner.py:128-133`) only consults the probe when `device == "auto"`; explicit `cuda` returns `"cuda"` verbatim. `DeviceContext.effective` (`device.py:39-41`) likewise returns explicit devices verbatim. cpu/mps unchanged (`test_healthcheck_cpu_device_ok_without_cuda`, `test_explicit_cpu_and_mps_pass_through`).

### 2. "auto" NEVER reaches wasb_infer.py — PASS (and load-bearing)
Confirmed `wasb_infer.py:682` declares `--device ... choices=["cuda","mps","cpu"]`, so a literal `auto` would make argparse exit rc=2. M4 resolves it before argv on **both** entry orderings:
- run_predict builds `--device` from `device = self._effective_device()` (`native_wasb_runner.py:221-224`), never `self.cfg.device`.
- Healthcheck-first: cache set at `:176`, reused with no 2nd probe. Healthcheck-skipped: `_effective_device`→`_cuda_is_available` lazily probes (`:122-126`). Either way argv gets cuda/cpu. Pinned by `test_run_predict_auto_threads_cpu_when_no_gpu` (`assert "auto" not in argv`) and `test_run_predict_auto_threads_cuda_when_gpu`. The two metadata sinks (`fusion_hybrid.py:87`, `wasb_hybrid.py:46`) only record `cfg.device` for reporting — they never build argv.

### 3. PROBE CACHING & CORRECTNESS — PASS
- Coherent: healthcheck sets `self._cuda_available = "cuda True" in out` (`:176`) for every device once torch imports, from a real probe; `_cuda_is_available` (`:122-126`) reuses it (None→probe-once memoization). GPU availability can't change within a process, so no staleness. Production always runs healthcheck before run_predict on the same instance (`trajectory_hybrid.py:228,239`), so the lazy path is the test/direct-API case only.
- `_probe_cuda` (`:112-120`) treats python-missing/timeout (caught `FileNotFoundError`/`TimeoutExpired` → `False`), import-error/rc!=0 (`res.returncode == 0 and ...` → `False`) all as "no cuda" → auto→cpu, never a crash. Pinned by `test_healthcheck_caches_probe_for_run_predict` (asserts `spy.call_count` stays 1) and `test_run_predict_explicit_cuda_does_not_probe` (`r._cuda_available is None`).

### 4. EDGE CASES — PASS
- `auto` + torch import fails (rc!=0) in healthcheck: returns False early at `:173-174`, `_cuda_available` stays None, production aborts. If healthcheck is skipped, the lazy `_probe_cuda` subprocess also fails → resolves to `cpu`, then the inference subprocess fails and run_predict returns `None` gracefully (`:238-240`). No crash.
- `mps`: passthrough, no probe, no warning (`device.py` mps branch; `test_explicit_cpu_and_mps_pass_through`).
- Warning fires only on real fallback: `:182 if ctx.fell_back` (`device.py:43-46`) is True only for auto-without-cuda. Pinned by `test_healthcheck_auto_falls_back_to_cpu_with_warning`.

### 5. TEST GAPS / OS — PASS
All five contracts are pinned by a failing-on-regression test: explicit-cuda-strict, auto→cpu, auto→cuda, no-probe-for-explicit, cache-reuse (enumerated above). `DeviceContext` frozen-ness (`test_is_frozen` → `FrozenInstanceError`) and all three properties (`effective`/`fell_back`/`cuda_required_but_missing`) covered in `tests/test_device_context.py`. No OS-dependent assertions — all subprocesses are mocked and Windows paths are normalized (`run_predict :198`); the suite passed here on win32.

### Nits (non-blocking, optional)
- **nit** — Unknown device string (e.g. `"gpu"`): passes healthcheck (not "cuda", not "auto" → no gate) and only fails later at `wasb_infer.py` argparse (rc=2 → run_predict returns None). This is pre-existing behavior M4 didn't regress, but since `DeviceContext` is now the device-policy seam, a future hardening could validate `requested ∈ {auto,cuda,mps,cpu}` in healthcheck for a clearer early error. Not required for M4.
- **nit** — `_probe_cuda` (`native_wasb_runner.py:115`) and healthcheck (`:165`) use two slightly different print formats, but both yield the `"cuda True"` substring the parsers key on (verified). Harmless duplication; a shared probe-code constant would remove the chance of them drifting apart.

No edits made (review only).
